MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」 直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market）」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於 2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至「邊境市場（Frontier Market
）」，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但 MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但 MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低

觸及 4,907.37 點。雖然 20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度下跌 1.22%
，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」，原則上將不再納入 MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外。相關改革有助改

善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續，以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證，我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至 2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異，區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響，以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元

政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模，轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過 AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對 AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化，政治事件不等於 AI 產業成長趨勢反轉：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為 AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以 AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若 AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者，利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對 AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

美國經濟維持穩健擴張，通膨壓力略升溫，Fed 維持利率不變，期中選舉進入
最後衝刺階段 
經濟 
⚫ 美國第三季實質 GDP 季增年率(初值)由上季的 2.2%升至 3.4%，成長主
要受民間消費、企業投資及庫存回補帶動。其中個人消費貢獻 2.52 個百
分點、私人投資貢獻 3.27 個百分點，惟淨出口拖累 3.32 個百分點，整
體經濟仍維持穩健擴張。 
⚫ 9 月美國新屋銷售年化 67.6 萬戶，月減 0.6%、年減 5.3%，主要是中西
部及南部地區銷售下滑拖累整體表現。房價雖較上月回升，但仍低於去
年同期；供給月數 8.4 個月，顯示市場仍呈供給充裕、需求偏弱格局。 
⚫ 美國 9 月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數年增率由 3.4%升至 3.6%，核心
PCE 物價指數年增率亦由 3.0%升至 3.1%，通膨再度升溫。儘管實質所
得受物價上漲影響而下滑，消費支出仍持續成長，反映家庭需求及內需
動能仍具韌性。 
⚫ 10 月 FOMC 會議以 10 比 2 決議將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%
至 4.0%。決策聲明指出，美國經濟活動表現強勁，就業市場變化不大，
通膨持續高於 2%目標，將依後續數據評估適當政策立場，以確保通膨
回歸目標。兩名委員主張升息 1 碼，顯示內部對通膨風險仍存分歧。主
席 Kevin Warsh 強調將防止能源價格上漲擴散至整體物價，並宣布 2027
年起部分 FOMC 會議改為策略討論會議，以強化長期政策研議。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 26 日路透社民調顯示，川普施政支持率 32%，略高於前週；國會
選舉政黨支持度則由民主黨 47%領先共和黨 36%。截至 10 月 31 日，
提前及郵寄投票已逾 5,100 萬人。整體而言，期中選舉結果將影響未來
國會版圖、施政推動及貿易政策方向，備受市場關注。 
短評 
⚫ 美國第三季 GDP 成長優於前季，主要受民間消費、企業投資及庫存回
補帶動，顯示內需與企業活動仍具韌性；惟新屋銷售持續疲弱、房市需
求未明顯改善，反映高利率環境仍抑制部分經濟活動。另一方面，PCE
通膨再度升溫，核心通膨降幅有限，顯示物價壓力仍存。在經濟穩健且
通膨高於目標情況下，Fed 維持利率不變並釋出偏鷹訊號，後續貨幣政
策料將維持審慎，持續關注通膨與就業數據變化。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.11.2 
一、 美國—經濟維持穩健擴張，通膨壓力略升溫，Fed 維持利率不變，期中選
舉進入最後衝刺階段 
2026 年第三季美國實質國內生產毛額(GDP)季增年率初值為 3.4%，高於第二
季之 2.2%，整體景氣持續穩健擴張。就成長貢獻而言，個人消費支出仍為經濟成
長主要支柱，貢獻 2.52 個百分點，其中服務消費及商品消費分別貢獻 1.89 個百
分點及 0.63 個百分點，反映民間需求維持穩健；私人投資則貢獻 3.27 個百分點，
為本季成長最大亮點，其中固定投資貢獻 1.66 個百分點，而企業庫存回補亦額外
貢獻 1.61 個百分點。對外貿易方面，淨出口拖累經濟成長 3.32 個百分點，其中
出口減少造成 0.68 個百分點負面影響；進口增加則拖累 2.64 個百分點。政府消
費支出與投資則受聯邦政府非國防支出增加帶動，貢獻 0.90 個百分點。此外，第
三季個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數及核心 PCE 物價指數分別年增 2.1%及 2.5%，
均低於第二季水準。整體而言，美國第三季經濟成長主要受民間消費、企業投資
及庫存回補支撐，雖貿易逆差擴大對 GDP 形成明顯拖累，但內需表現仍具韌性，
顯示美國經濟基本面維持穩健。 
2026 年 9 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整後年化戶數為 67.6 萬戶，較上月減
少 0.6%，並較去年同期下降 5.3%，顯示在高利率環境下住宅需求仍較去年疲弱。
地區別表現分歧，其中東北部及西部分別較上月增加 28.0%及 21.4%，惟中西部
及南部分別減少 7.3%及 6.6%，尤其占全美銷售比重最大的南部地區回落，成為
拖累整體銷售的主因。價格方面，9 月新屋銷售中位價為 40.04 萬美元，較上月
上升 3.9%，但較去年同期下跌 3.9%，儘管房價高於上月，惟仍面臨修正壓力。
供給方面，9 月底待售新屋數量為 47.6 萬戶，較上月減少 1.0%，較去年同期減少
2.3%；按目前銷售速度計算，供給月數為 8.4 個月，低於上月的 8.5 個月，但高
於去年同期的 8.2 個月。整體而言，9 月美國新屋銷售較前月小幅下滑且年減幅
度擴大，反映購屋需求仍未明顯改善；雖然部分地區銷售回升且庫存略有下降，
但供給月數仍維持相對偏高水準，顯示新屋市場仍處於供給較充裕、需求偏弱的
格局，後續仍須觀察利率走勢及就業市場變化對住宅需求的影響。 
2026 年 9 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增 0.5%，高於 8 月之 0.3%，

年增率則由 3.4%升至 3.6%；扣除食品及能源後之核心 PCE 月增率維持於 0.3%，
年增率由 3.0%微升至 3.1%，能源價格回升及服務價格上漲持續對整體物價形成
支撐，通膨再度升溫。另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)亦月增
0.2%，個人消費支出則月增 0.5%，增幅高於所得成長，顯示家庭消費需求仍具韌
性；惟受物價上漲影響，實質可支配所得月減 0.3%，實質個人消費支出則持平，
反映名目支出增加部分已被物價上升所抵銷。整體而言，美國 9 月消費活動維持
穩健，內需仍為經濟成長提供支撐，但通膨壓力較前月略有升溫，物價回落至聯
準會 2%目標之過程仍具挑戰，後續仍須持續關注能源價格變化、核心通膨走勢
及消費動能對聯準會貨幣政策方向之影響。 
聯準會(Fed)於 2026 年 10 月 FOMC 會議以 10 比 2 表決結果，決定將聯邦基
金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%至 4.00%不變，符合市場預期；謹將本次會議聲明
及主席 Kevin Warsh 記者會重點摘錄如下：(一)聯準會認為美國經濟活動持續以
強勁步調擴張，較前次聲明所稱之「穩健擴張」進一步上修對經濟表現之評價；
儘管地緣政治情勢使經濟前景不確定性仍偏高，但國內支出維持韌性，生產力成
長與資本投資表現強勁，就業增幅大致與勞動力成長同步，失業率變動不大，顯
示整體經濟基本面仍具相當韌性；(二)通膨仍高於聯準會 2%目標，委員會表示需
累積更多經濟與物價資訊，以評估適當政策立場，確保通膨適時回歸 2%目標，
並重申將致力維持物價穩定；(三)本次決議出現兩張反對票，聯準會理事 Lisa Cook
及克里夫蘭聯邦準備銀行總裁 Beth Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，反映部分委員對通
膨風險仍抱持較為謹慎態度；(四)主席 Kevin Warsh 於記者會表示，近期聯邦公
開市場委員會討論焦點多集中於物價穩定，特別是在近期能源及相關價格再度上
揚情況下，聯準會有責任避免個別產業價格上漲擴散至整體經濟，並確保通膨預
期維持穩定；(五)Warsh 並宣布調整 FOMC 會議安排，2027 年仍將維持每年 8 次
會議，但其中 1 次將改為專門討論貨幣政策策略之會議，不進行利率表決或發布
政策聲明，並預期 2028 年將有 2 次會議採取此模式，以騰出更多時間進行長期
政策與重大議題研議。整體而言，本次會議雖維持利率不變，但聯準會仍認為通
膨問題尚未完全解決，後續政策方向仍將取決於通膨、勞動市場及整體經濟數據
表現。 
根據 10 月 26 日路透社最新民調，總統川普施政支持率為 32%，較前一週之
31%略為回升；在國會選舉政黨支持度方面，47%的登記選民表示傾向支持民主

選舉結果。另一方面，根據 10 月 31 日統計，已有超過 5,100 萬名選民透過提前

整體而言，本次期中選舉結果除將影響國會兩院席次分布外，亦可能牽動川普政
府後續施政推動、預算協商及貿易政策方向，受到國際市場高度關注。 
二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期暫停升息，12 月升息機率較高 
 歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率為 0.4%，但有部份是受到愛爾蘭經濟成長率大幅
波動的扭曲，若剔除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率僅約 0.2%。該季受到
物價壓力上升、地緣政治緊張局勢等因素影響，對消費及投資需求構成負面因素，
但世足賽以及德國財政支出擴張的支持下，仍使歐元區經濟保持正向表現。就國
別而言，德、法、義、西 GDP 季增率分別為 1.2%、-0.1%、0.8%、2.7%；其中德
國經濟活動受到財政擴張的提振；法國的消費支出雖強勁，但受到投資減緩和進
口增加拖累；義大利製造業產出萎縮拖累成長表現；西班牙在住宅營建熱潮的支
持下，經濟活動持續優於其他國家，但歐盟 NGEU 計畫到期以及政府財政整頓力
道，西班牙後續經濟動能可能會逐漸放緩。 
 歐洲央行在 10 月 29 日宣布維持政策利率不變，主要是近期歐元區各國殖利
率普遍上升，對經濟造成限制性因素，且不確定性可能會隨著時間的推移而累積，
從而拖累經濟成長。不過，歐洲央行仍認為近期高漲的能源價格對通膨的壓力相
當大，使央行考慮進一步採取貨幣政策措施因應。因此，除非經濟成長受到超出
預期的衝擊、能源價格下跌或金融環境急劇收緊，一般預期歐洲央行在 12 月的
政策會議將會宣布升息一碼。 
三、日本—日本央行暫停升息，通膨壓力續存，升息預期升溫 
 9 月工業生產月增 2.2%，扭轉 8 月受天災造成生產中斷而下滑的情勢，從細
項來看，汽車、電子產品及生產機械產量均呈回升，其中生產機械已連續第四個
月成長，反映 AI 相關資本支出需求持續暢旺；庫存月增 2.5%，石化產品庫存亦
逐步回補。展望未來，製造商預估 10 月及 11 月產量將分別月增 5.1%及 0.8%，

顯示在 AI 及資本支出需求、汽車復產與庫存回補支撐下，短期工業生產動能可
望維持強勁。 
 9 月零售月減 0.7%，主要受雨季抑制家庭外出消費，以及先前受新規上路前
換機需求及汽車稅減免政策帶動的耐久財消費動能逐漸減弱，致整體消費動能偏
弱；惟服裝及一般商品銷售受假期延長與節慶需求支撐而有所回升。另 10 月消
費者信心指數下降 0.4 點至 35.0，連續第二個月下滑，反映消費者對物價上漲及
實質購買力下降的憂慮加深，預期未來一年通膨率超過 2%的消費者比重亦升至
79.8%。展望未來，隨天候等暫時性不利因素逐漸消退，加上所得成長仍具韌性，
第四季消費動能可望回升；惟通膨持續升溫，加上汽油補貼逐步退場，恐進一步
侵蝕購買力，為消費復甦的下行風險。 
 10 月東京核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，石油相關成本上漲已由核心商品逐步擴散至服
務價格，食品及生鮮食品價格亦偏強。雖目前能源、汽油補貼等政府措施仍抑制
通膨，但隨 12 月相關補貼逐步退場，成本壓力將進一步反映於商品及服務價格，
預期至 2026 年底潛在通膨仍可能高於央行的 2%目標，通膨黏性值得持續關注。 
 日本央行於 10 月 29、30 日召開貨幣政策會議，維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，
主要係仍需觀察先前升息的累積效果，但整體政策訊號仍偏鷹派。本次決議以 8：
1 通過，唯一反對的政策委員高田創主張再升息 1 碼至 1.5%，反映央行內部對通
膨上行風險仍具警戒。日本央行指出通膨已大致達到與 2%通膨目標相符的水準，
同時將 2027 財年的核心 CPI(不含生鮮食品)預測由 2.4%上調至 2.6%，顯示目前
通膨情勢仍支持貨幣政策進一步正常化。央行總裁表示，目前金融環境仍屬寬鬆，
未來若經濟及物價走勢符合預期，仍將適時調升政策利率，市場因而持續關注後
續會議再度升息的可能性。此外，日本央行亦決定自 2027 年 1 月起，將 ETF 出
售規模由約 3,300 億日圓提高至 8,600 億日圓，加速資產負債表正常化進程。 
四、中國—工業部門成本轉嫁能力有限，使利潤率持續萎縮 
1-9 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 15.5、13.1%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-8 月)大增 8.9 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 4.5 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 17.5%、79.7%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增 21%、103.8%)、化學原料

府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰、菲進出口創歷史新高，泰國央行按兵不動以支撐經濟 
(1) 新加坡：9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率小幅回落至 2.2%、核心通膨率自 8
月的 2.2%降至 2%，反映交通運輸（7.4%）、服裝鞋帽（2.1%）及家庭耐用品
和服務（0.2%）價格漲幅顯著放緩，此外，由於行業競爭壓力，資訊通信技術
服務價格的進一步回落也抑制整體服務價格的上漲。隨著 11 月電力和天然氣
價格下調生效，預計 2026 年第四季核心通膨將進一步放緩。 
(2) 泰國：9 月出口額達 384.4 億美元、年增 24.0%，創歷史新高，主要得益於工
業產品出口成長 25.4%，其中，以電腦及相關設備（59.4%）、電話及零件
（117.5%）及電路板（45.9%）成長最為顯著，對美國出口額年增 50.1%，對
中國和日本的出口額分別成長 18.4%和 19.0%。9 月進口額 443 億美元、年增
50.4%，創歷史新高，因出口導向製造業生產需求及全球能源價格的上漲，主
要類別進口額均有所成長，其中，資本貨物(電腦、機械零件、電路板等)進口
大增 92.3%，燃料、原材料和中間產品進口增幅均逾 4 成、運輸設備在飛機與
航空設備進口大增 12 倍下成長 24%。9 月貿易逆差大幅擴大至 59 億美元，累
計 1 至 9 月，貿易逆差達 437 億美元，出口額成長 19.6%、進口額成長 37.8%。
泰國央行 10 月會議維持基準利率於 1%不變，維持寬鬆的貨幣政策立場，以
支持經濟復甦的不平衡。經濟預估方面，強於預期的出口以及與科技和人工智
慧週期相關的投資，預估 2026 年和 2027 年經濟成長率將分別成長 2.4%和
2.1%，然而，高額的家庭債務、脆弱的勞動市場和疲軟的購買力繼續抑制私人
消費，使經濟成長仍低於潛在水平。通膨方面，受能源價格上漲和聖嬰現象的
影響，預計通膨率在 2027 年初將出現短暫上升，之後隨著國內需求的疲軟而
回落，2026 年和 2027 年整體通膨率平均分別為 2.1%和 1.3%。

(3) 菲律賓:9 月出口額達 98.7 億美元、年增 35.6%，主要由電子產品出口成長 72%
所帶動，占總出口額達 70.1%，其中半導體元件、辦公設備、電子設備及零件

六、台灣—AI 動能續強，經濟成長動能穩健 
9 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，較上月增加 1 分，燈號
續呈紅燈，其中工業及服務業加班工時，受惠 AI 相關產業生產動能維持熱絡，
由綠燈轉呈黃紅燈，其餘八項燈號均維持不變。另領先與同時指標皆呈上升，顯
示國內經濟動能維持穩健。 
主計總處 10 月 30 日公布台灣第三季經濟成長率為 14.36%，主要受 AI 需求
強勁帶動出口及企業投資大幅成長，民間消費亦維持熱絡，顯示外需動能正逐步
向內需擴散，整體經濟呈現內外皆熱。展望未來，AI 需求、半導體相關投資及企
業資本支出持續擴張，加上年底消費旺季，可望支撐短期景氣表現；惟今年經濟
高速成長墊高比較基期，明年增速料將放緩，後續仍須關注 AI 需求延續性、成
長動能向傳統產業擴散情形及金融市場波動等不確定因素。

2026 年 10 月 30 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）予測

～ほぼゼロ成長も、緩やかな景気回復は継続～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田 隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表する 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.0%（前期比年
率換算＋0.1%）と予測する。設備投資や政府消費の増加に支えられ、かろうじて 4 四半期連続のプラス成長を
維持したものの、個人消費や輸出が前期の一時的な押し上げ要因の剥落もあって減少したことで、ほぼゼロ成長
にとどまったと見込まれる。なお、輸入が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は大きめのマイナスと
なるものの、公的在庫のプラス寄与により、おおむね相殺されたと考えられる。 
また、名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.9%（年率換算＋3.8%）となったと見込まれる。コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進
む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比＋2.7%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.9%）と高めの伸びが続いた。 
今回、7～9 月期の実質 GDP はほぼゼロ成長にとどまる見込みだが、イラン情勢の悪化という逆風を踏まえれ
ば、国内景気は底堅さを維持している。先行きについては、輸入コストの高止まりに伴う価格転嫁の進展が景気
の重しとなる可能性はあるものの、良好な雇用・所得環境や企業業績の改善を背景に内需の回復基調が維持さ
れることなどから、国内景気は緩やかな回復を続けると期待される。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」により作成

### Page 2

2 / 2

需要項目別の動向は以下の通りである。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.1%） 
 良好な所得環境が維持される中、消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かったものの、前期に耐久財
が自動車やエアコンを中心に一時的な要因によって増加した反動が生じたとみられる。価格転
嫁の進展による物価上昇、地震や台風といった自然災害の発生も重しとなった可能性がある。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－0.3%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が横ばいとなる中、建築コストが上昇していることもあり、住
宅投資は小幅ながら減少が続いたとみられる。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋0.9%） 
 企業の投資意欲は省力化やデジタル需要などに支えられて堅調であり、設備投資は 3 四半期
ぶりに増加したと見込まれる。機械設備投資の供給側統計である資本財総供給（除く輸送機械）
も 7～9 月期は前期比＋5.3%と増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度－0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫などの積み増しを進めた反動もあ
り、7～9 月期はマイナス寄与になったと考えられる。 
 なお、公的在庫については、政府による代替調達の進展により石油備蓄の減少ペースが大きく鈍
化したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントとプラス寄与になったとみられる。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費支出（前期比＋0.3%） 
 高齢化が進む中、医療費・介護費を中心に底堅く推移したと予測する。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－2.7%） 
 建築・土木コストの上昇が続いている中、7～8 月の建設総合統計（出来高ベース：公共）は弱
めの動きとなったことから、実質公共投資は 2 四半期連続で減少したと見込まれる。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.5%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.5%、実質輸入：同＋1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.5%と減少したと予測する。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受
けて増加した一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動
で減少したとみられる。 
 輸入は前期比＋1.7%と増加したと予測する。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただ
し、サービスについては減少した可能性がある。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.5%ポイントとマイナスになったと見込ま
れる。

Industry View In-Line
4Q26 iPhone builds unchanged: We maintain our 4Q26 iPhone build forecast at
72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y). While builds are expected to increase sequentially,
volumes remain below last year’s level, with softer legacy-model production,
including the iPhone 16/17e, weighing on the outlook. Our 2H26 build forecast for

the new iPhone 18 family remains unchanged at 71M units (-21% y/y), with the y/y
comparison also affected by one fewer SKU launched than last year. Within this, we
continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo builds. We will continue to monitor the
production ramp and sell-through data for potential upside or downside to our build
estimates. Our full-year forecast implies CY26 iPhone shipments of 234M units
(+3% y/y), with modest growth for the year despite the softer 4Q y/y outlook.

4Q26 iPad builds unchanged; year-end production outlook remains subdued: We
maintain our 4Q26 iPad build forecast at 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y), implying
continued pressure on production volumes into year-end. Our full-year estimate
remains 46.8M units (-14% y/y), with the sharper y/y contraction in 4Q pointing to a
weaker finish to the year.

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

Document 154: 261117_ubs_BIS-control

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_ubs_BIS-control.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
UBS Global I/O Semiconductors
New US Export Controls on Cloud Access;
Assessing the Impact
Summary Thoughts
The US Dept. of Commerce Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS) posted a new rule
addressing remote access to advanced semiconductors. Following discussions with
industry experts (including a former Commerce Dept. official), we believe the rule  is
essentially attempting to accomplish the same thing as the AI Diffusion Rule issued by

the Biden Administration, but never put into effect. We would note that the rule was
"written by, and at the direction of the  Department of Treasury" - atypical for such a
rule, according to our experts. While we can't say for sure that this is in direct response
to the NVDA situation, the timing seems highly coincidental and we highlighted this risk
here and in our estimate cuts yesterday morning. We would make a few key points:
 Demand impact is hard to assess, but could be significant. Current export

control laws have not required a license  for shipments to key countries such as
Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia, and Singapore (though we note  there is little room
for data centers in Singapore). As a result, we have seen a large increase in data
center construction in Malaysia in particular with recent press reports from the
NYT and Economist  (here and here) suggesting ~2GW of new data centers now
online in a single province in Malaysia with another ~10GW currently under

construction, and  total approved volume up to ~25GW with backing from both US
and China hyperscalers. On this basis, it seems fair to assume  a  run-rate for
shipments into Malaysia alone of a few GW/yr and the NYT article indicated that
about half of this is going to China-domiciled  companies. There is a carve-out in
the new rule for online retailers and social media companies but we would think
that US companies would err on the side of conservatism unless they can be

continuously assured of the end customer. Ergo, it is possible that this new rule
could strip 1-2GW out of current demand, with most of this likely being from
NVDA. At $30B+/GW, this is roughly consistent with our estimate of a 10%
demand impact, though NVDA should still be able to divert these shipments to
other customers to mitigate any near-term impact so we see this as more of a
longer-term issue especially if powered shell permitting gets more restrictive in the

US.
 Odds of MATCH Act passage have gone up, but timing remains a key Q. We
have written in the past about  MATCH  here and here. While the US may not want
to enforce additional export controls on the heels of this new cloud access rule,
our conversations with experts marked a clear shift in tone around the odds of the
MATCH Act passing vs. prior months - with the legislation potentially being
attached to the NDAA in this upcoming Congress. That being said, an expert firmly

believes that the disparity between what ASML, TEL, and other foreign equipment
suppliers currently sell into China vs. US SPE suppliers is not all that significant,
making the rule more of a point of tension with US allies such as the Netherlands
and Japan. Second, the goal of this legislation would be "level the playing field"
using the FDPR (Foreign Direct Product Rule) but there is a question of who would
determine which tools sold by the Dutch and Japanese suppliers are “equivalent”

to those currently restricted for US equipment suppliers. Third, a contemplated
ban on immersion DUV would make supporting trailing-edge fabs and lines in
China quite challenging, with potential spillover effects across the industry, even
outside China. Fourth, it raises the question of what incentives, if any, the Dutch
and Japanese governments would have to comply. Related to this, an expert
indicated that the use of FDPR by the USG could be more upsetting than tariffs

### Page 2

directly impacted by this rule, but in our coverage, we see KLAC being the only one
really indirectly negatively affected by the passage of this rule given its high attach
to litho process steps (and others affected to a lesser extent, though less litho
could in fact ripple to fewer dep/etch tools).
Read Across
We see NVDA as the most directly impacted by this rule, though the entire supply chain,
from memory through SPE, would ultimately be negatively affected by the closure of

such a large demand corridor. That said, we could argue that, over the longer term,
approving  the MATCH Act could benefit memory suppliers outside China by
constraining domestic Chinese capacity additions.

future course of unit sales, ASPs, and revenues.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

優群 3Q26 獲利低於預期，主要受筆電需求疲弱、稼動率下降及原
物料成本上漲影響，預期 4Q26 營運仍將承壓。惟隨 Google 伺服
器新案及網通新產品於 2027 年放量，加上高單價新產品陸續導
入，營收與獲利可望重返成長。研究部下修優群 2026、2027 年稅
後 EPS 至 10.06 元（原估 11.31 元）及 11.83 元（原估 14.30 
元）。考量短期利空已逐步反映於股價，現價對應 2027 年本益比
僅 8.8 倍，低於歷史區間下緣，且預估殖利率約 7.6%，具備下檔
支撐及評價修復空間。研究部以 2027 年 EPS 為評價基礎，給予 
12 倍本益比，目標價 140 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 筆電需求疲弱、稼動率下降，3Q26 獲利低於預期 
優群 3Q26 營收 9.75 億元（+2.9% QoQ、−13.8% YoY），低於原
估 10.90 億元，主因筆電客戶持續調整出貨，SO-DIMM 與 M.2 營
收分別年減 36% 及 20%，抵銷伺服器與新產品成長貢獻。受營收
不如預期、產能利用率下降及原物料成本上漲影響，毛利率降至 
46.1%（−1.5ppt QoQ、−5.1ppt YoY），低於原估 47.4%；營益率
降至 27.8%，低於原估 29.9%。稅後淨利 2.55 億元（+18.9% QoQ、
−32.8% YoY），稅後 EPS 2.84 元，低於原估 3.42 元。

表一、優群 3Q26 獲利檢討及 4Q26 獲利修正 
項目

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 筆電需求持續疲弱，下修 4Q26 獲利預估 
優群 10 月營收 2.58 億元（−22.9% MoM、−25.9% YoY），主要受
筆電客戶持續調整出貨影響。預期 11、12 月出貨將較 10 月回
升，惟整體需求仍偏弱，產能利用率下降將持續壓抑毛利率表現。
考量筆電需求修正幅度超出預期，研究部下修 4Q26 營收預估至 
8.80 億元（−9.8% QoQ、−15.5% YoY，原估 10.26 億元），毛利
率由原估 47.8% 下修至 45.4%，稅後 EPS 由 2.69 元下修至 
2.02 元（−29.3% QoQ、−37.9% YoY）。

3. CSP 伺服器與網通新產品放量，2027 年營運結構持續優化 
隨 AI 推論及企業運算需求成長，伺服器對記憶體容量、頻寬及傳
輸效能的要求持續提升，帶動 DDR5 R-DIMM 朝高速化與高容量發
展。相較傳統 U-DIMM，R-DIMM 對訊號完整性及可靠度要求較高，
連接器製程與品質門檻亦隨之提升，有利高單價產品需求成長。優
群持續拓展 Meta 與 Google 等 CSP 客戶訂單，2026 年 R-DIMM 
累計出貨量已超越 2025 年全年，且高單價 R-DIMM 占比持續提
升，有助優化產品組合。其中，Google 伺服器新案預計於 1Q27 在
越南廠量產，除帶動 Long-DIMM 出貨成長，亦有助提升越南廠稼
動率並改善規模經濟，研究部預估 2027 年 Long-DIMM 營收成長 
30.9% YoY。網通方面，優群持續拓展既有筆電與伺服器以外的通
訊設備應用，新產品已於 3Q26 開始量產，預期 2027 年隨客戶訂
單增加及新應用拓展，出貨規模持續擴大。搭配 CAMM2、CCBB 等
新產品貢獻，研究部預估 2027 年 Others 營收成長 33.8% YoY，
成為營運結構轉型的重要成長來源。相較之下，傳統筆電 SO-DIMM 
營收預估衰退 2.4% YoY，顯示營運成長動能逐步由筆電轉向伺服
器與網通應用，有助降低對筆電景氣的依賴。

圖一、優群產品營收比重（2024～2027F）

資料來源：優群，統一投顧預估

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 記憶體模組化與可維修設計趨勢，帶動高單價產品滲透 
隨筆電朝輕薄化、高速傳輸及可維修設計發展，連接器規格持續升
級，帶動高單價產品導入。記憶體方面，傳統 SO-DIMM 受限於模
組高度與訊號傳輸需求，部分高階筆電轉向焊接式 LPDDR 設計，
壓抑傳統連接器需求；CAMM2 則兼具低矮化、高速傳輸及模組可更
換等優勢，提供新一代記憶體連接方案。優群 CAMM2 目前月出貨
約 5～10 萬顆，HP 約 10 款高階機種正進行認證，預計 1H27 量
產，隨模組供應改善及新機種導入，可望擴大出貨規模。Type-C 方
面，隨 USB-C 成為筆電充電與資料傳輸的主要介面，接口維修成
本逐漸受到品牌重視。優群推出可獨立拆卸的兩件式 Type-C，無
須因接口損壞而更換整片主機板，單價約為傳統產品的兩倍，目前
已導入聯想機種，HP 預計於 2027 年加入。此外，Metal Bar 微
型沖壓件持續由筆電拓展至記憶體及 SiP 模組，憑藉精密沖壓與
微型化製程能力，擴大高毛利產品應用。研究部預估 2027 年 
Metal Bar 營收成長約 18% YoY，隨高單價新產品滲透率提升，有
助優化產品組合並支撐毛利率回升。

5. 筆電需求修正超出預期，下修 2026 年獲利，2027 年重返成
長 
考量筆電需求疲弱、客戶持續調整出貨，加上稼動率下降及原物料
成本上漲壓抑獲利，研究部下修優群 2026 年營收預估至 37.50 
億元（−9.6% YoY，原估 40.11 億元），其中 SO-DIMM 與 M.2 營
收分別衰退 29% 及 17% YoY，為主要拖累；毛利率由原估 47.9% 
下修至 47.0%，稅後淨利預估 9.03 億元（−21.0% YoY），稅後 
EPS 由 11.31 元下修至 10.06 元。 
展望 2027 年，隨 CSP 伺服器新案放量、網通業務擴張及高單價
新產品持續貢獻，研究部預估 Long-DIMM、Metal Bar 及其他產品
營收分別成長約 31%、18% 及 34% YoY，帶動全年營收回升至 
41.12 億元（+9.7% YoY，原估 46.67 億元）。隨營收規模擴大、
稼動率提升及產品組合改善，毛利率預估回升至 47.6%，稅後淨利 
10.54 億元（+16.8% YoY），稅後 EPS 11.83 元（+16.8% YoY，
原估 14.30 元）。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 短期利空已逐步反映，低評價與高殖利率浮現投資價值 
考量筆電需求疲弱及短期獲利下修，優群股價已大幅修正，現價對
應 2027 年本益比僅 8.8 倍，低於歷史 10～20 倍區間下緣，反
映短期營運利空已逐步消化。此外，優群近五年現金配息率約 78%
～79%，以研究部預估 2026 年 EPS 10.06 元推算，現金股利約 
7.9 元，按 11/18 收盤價 104.5 元計算，預估殖利率約 7.6%，
提供下檔支撐。展望 2027 年，隨 CSP 伺服器與網通新業務放量，
營收及獲利可望重返成長，帶動評價修復。綜合考量短期利空已逐
步反映、獲利成長動能回升及殖利率支撐，研究部以 2027 年 EPS 
為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益比，目標價 140 元，投資評等由中
立調升至買進。

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

policy this week, in part due to price pressures
emanating from the Middle East."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the
past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but
also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may
be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and comments made in
the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this
was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

### Page 1

18 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
DO ITALIAN HOUSEHOLDS HAVE WHAT IT TAKES TO FINANCE EUROPE’S NEXT IDEAS?
Andrea d’Aquino
Between 2019 and 2025, US value added grew at twice the pace of Europe’s, driven largely by technology and labour productivity 1,2. Yet Europe
does not lack saving to finance a similar development: over the past ten years, Euro area gross national saving exceeded domestic investment
by about €300 bn a year, equivalent to 15% of productive investment 3, and in 2024-2025 gross capital outflows to the United States averaged

€785 billion a year 4. The situation described therefore creates a “European paradox”, where household particularly save but do not take on so
much risk to foster innovation as in the US.
Safety versus risk: how Italian and European households differ
In Q2 2026, Euro area households’ gross financial wealth grew by 6.5% y/y to approximately €37 trillion, of which just over 30% is held in
currency and deposits (€11,165 billion), compared with about 10% in the United States. Italy has the lowest share of currency and deposits

among the major Euro area economies (see Chart 1). In Q2 2026, Italian households’ financial wealth grew by 6.3% y/y, in line with the
Euro area, reaching €6,609 billion, of which 24.2% is held in currency and deposits (about €1,598 billion), compared with 28.3% in France
(€2,151 billion), 32% in Spain (€1,168 billion) and 36% in Germany (€3,660 billion).
1 Fabio Panetta; Il risparmio nella società che cambia, Banca d’Italia; 30th October 2026.
2 Luisa Carpinelli et al.; The Great Divide: productivity dynamics in the United States and euro area after the pandemic; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 924, Banca d’Italia; April

2025.
3 Productive investments are gross fixed investments minus residential construction.
4 It includes the item ‘other investments’, mainly made up of deposits, loans, and trade receivables.

Chart of the Week / 18 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Italian risk is tied up mostly in unlisted firms
Judging by this metric, European households’ risk appetite is lower than that of their US counterparts. Another metric to look at: the listed
shares. They account for 5% of Euro area gross financial wealth 5 (3.1% in Italy, 3.7% in France, 6.6% in Spain, 7.2% in Germany). However,
once holdings in unlisted companies are included, the share of risk capital rises to 15% in the Euro area, 34% in the United States and

more than 20% in Italy.
A large part of the gap reflects the wider presence in Europe of unlisted firms, largely family-owned. In Italy in particular, unlisted shares
account for 17.1% of gross financial wealth, just over 7 pp above the Euro area average, compared with 12.4% in France, 7.7% in Spain and
2.6% in Germany (see Chart 2). Unlisted family firms are on average smaller and less productive than the system as a whole, especially
among medium-sized and large firms and in technology- and knowledge-intensive sectors 6.

Italian households’ risk capital is therefore largely held in forms that are hard to diversify and in firms far from the technological frontier,
while the risk capital reaching the youngest firms (less than 12 years old) is scarce, even though these firms are very active in innovation:
innovative start-ups account for just over 2% of young firms but hold about 44% of that category’s patents. Similarly, venture-capital-
backed firms are barely 0.2% of young firms but account for around 12% of total patents of the youngest firms 7.

Italian inheritances will largely pass on bricks
Investment choices are also linked to demographics: risk appetite tends to rise until adulthood and then declines with advancing age 8.
According to Banca d’Italia 9, in the coming years in Italy, those born between 1966 and 1975 will benefit from wealth transfers to a greater
extent than earlier cohorts: inheritances would account for around 40% of their wealth, compared with 30% for those born between 1951

and 1965. All else being equal, households expecting to receive an inheritance hold more financial assets. What will change hands is
mainly real assets, above all housing: in 2022 (latest available data), households whose reference person was born between 1941 and
1950 held on average around 62% of their total wealth in dwellings, 28% in financial assets and almost 10% in equity stakes in businesses.
As a result, once households receive the inheritance, their portfolios could become more tilted towards housing, which may lead to a

rebalancing towards financial assets.
The challenge for the Euro area in the coming years will be to press ahead with the creation of a single market for capital, in order to
provide greater investment opportunities and avert the “European savings paradox”.
andrea.aquino@bnpparibas.com

Document 337: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

After a frenetic week of tech-led selling that dragged the S&P
500 index nearly 9% lower, equity futures are marked 2%
higher today. Equity corrections have tended to be short, sharp
aﬀairs since a major rally started in 2022, and investors will be
hoping the worst is over. The challenge will be another hot US
inﬂation print this week. USD is set to hold gains

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
Equity markets are stabilising after a torrid week, but the dollar can hold onto
its gains
USD: Hawkish Fed undercurrent still a
dollar support
After several support measures from authorities across Asia, local
equity markets are stabilising. This has been helped in part by
seeing US equity futures marked 2% higher today. Notably, Friday's
US session did not see complete capitulation in equity markets,
with defensive sectors like consumer staples posting net gains on
the day. However, with a 34% weight in the S&P 500, the 7% fall in
the IT sector did most of the damage. Since the current major rally

in US equities started in 2022, corrections have tended to be short,

What would help stabilise equities would be a less aggressive
Federal Reserve. Over the weekend, US Treasury Secretary Scott

Any reversal in the likes of copper or precious metals may suggest

however, we expect the defensive currencies of the dollar, yen and

Swiss franc to stay in demand.
For today, US S&P PMI business conﬁdence data is expected to

correct much below the 103.35/50 area.

EUR: Clinging onto 1.10
Another remarkable feature of last week's FX price action was the
very narrow ranges seen in EUR/USD while US tech stocks tanked.
Nearly 30bp was priced out of the 2027 Fed tightening cycle last
week, but the dollar held its gains pretty well. We felt this was due
to the market already being relatively long dollars and a lot of
option strike congestion in the 1.0850/0950 area.

This week, EUR/USD has to face another high US October inﬂation
reading on Wednesday, and on Thursday, EU leaders should reach
a decision on the next European Central Bank President. According
to Polymarket, the contest is over, with Pablo Hernández de Cos
given a 96% chance of getting the job. We doubt the euro needs to
soften on this much-anticipated outcome and would expect the
Chief Economist position to go to a candidate from northern Europe

as a counterweight.

French business conﬁdence remaining in contraction territory, the
rest of the region's conﬁdence is expected to hold up pretty well.
We do not see a strong reason for this EUR/USD correction to go
much further than 1.1030 in the short term. But we are moving
closer to seasonal dollar weakness, and any close above 1.11 this
week would provide much relief to an otherwise weak EUR/USD

story.

Chris Turner
CHF: SNB turning a little more hawkish
Over the weekend, Swiss National Bank Vice President Antoine
Martin warned that inﬂation was picking up. He was probably

referring to the 1.5% year-on-year reading seen in October. But his
remarks do serve as a reminder about whether the SNB can keep
rates at zero during a global inﬂation crisis. Money markets price
unchanged SNB policy into year-end, but two hikes are priced in by
next September.
We doubt the SNB is ready to hike in December, but we do think it
will grow increasingly tolerant of Swiss franc strength, where the
franc plays an important role in monetary conditions for a small

open economy.
We see downside risks in EUR/CHF over the coming quarters and
could see a retest of 0.9125 over the coming weeks.

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Charts of the week: Oil’s disinflation effect

Falling oil and fuel prices likely to support disinflation in 2027; bonds are starting to offer greater diversification benefits 
Projected* y/y change in crude oil and gasoline prices by quarter     21- and 126-day rolling correlation of global equities and bonds

Source: US Energy Information Administration, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *EIA projections

Editorial 
Staying diversified, adding US mega caps 
Strategy summary. AI-related concerns continue to weigh on 
equities, especially with new US restrictions on third-country 
access to advanced American semiconductor chips. Renewed 
volatility across the AI ecosystem drove the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) back above 25. However, two major event risks this 
week turned out to be less concerning - Nvidia’s Q3 revenue 
and earnings exceeded expectations, while Anthropic’s record 
initial public offering was priced above its indicative range.  
Our quant models are not signalling strong buy signal yet. 
However, with VIX spiking above 25 and investor positioning 
and sentiment indicators suggesting limited downside, we see 
value emerging in the US mega caps segment following its 
recent underperformance. We add an opportunistic idea on the 
segment, which we see benefitting from scale, financial 
strength and potential industry consolidation.  
Opportunistically adding US megacaps. Asian equities led 
declines this week, led by Taiwan, China and Korea, after new 
US export controls targeted access to advanced AI computing 
through overseas data centres. Licensing requirements focus 
on capacity supplied to restricted customers, with additional 
controls affecting regional hubs. These measures increase 
compliance burdens and financing uncertainty, potentially 
weighing on AI capital expenditure. We expect the market’s AI 
capex estimates for 2027 to be cut towards our own more-
conservative projections. Established cloud operators 
(hyperscalers) may be better equipped to manage customer 
checks than smaller providers. We initiate an opportunistic idea 
on US mega caps as we see a catch-up opportunity, given their 
established franchises, strong cash generation and relatively 
resilient earnings prospects (see page 7 for details). 
Diversifying beyond the AI trade. Although positive cross 
asset and sector correlations returned this week, sector 
rotations earlier this month reinforces the importance of 
maintaining exposure to different drivers of equity returns. Our 
preferred US sectors remain technology, healthcare, financials 
and materials, combining technology participation with 
opportunities beyond AI. Beyond the US, we like Euro area and 
Japanese banks, which are benefitting from elevated interest 
rates, actively managed global dividend strategies for income 
opportunities, and Indian equities due to their low correlation 
with the AI trade. 
Allure of high dividend equities. While global dividend yields 
remain historically low, attractive pockets persist. The Swiss 
equity market yields almost 4%, versus 1.6% for global equities. 
Euro area (3.6%), Australia (3.5%) and Singapore (3.4%) are 
other leading dividend paying markets. Taiwan offers almost a 
2% yield, combining income with structural growth 
opportunities. We favour active management over simply 
chasing yield, avoiding value traps. Dividend equities can 
complement bonds within a diversified portfolio, balancing 
income opportunities with broader exposure across markets. 
Increasing diversification offered by bonds. The one-month 
correlation between global stocks and bonds has turned 
negative after more than a year. This means bonds are offering 
greater diversification benefits amid the equity drawdown. 
Minutes from the Fed’s October meeting suggest policymakers 
are likely to hike rates further. However, we remain dovish vs. 
markets on the Fed’s 12-month rates outlook (we see Fed 
target rate at 4.25% by end-2027 vs. market pricing above 
4.5%) as lower oil prices and fading tariff effects lead to 
noticeable disinflation by H2 2027.  Thus, we see particular 
value in the 3-7-year maturity bucket, with a preference for BB-
to-BBB segment corporate bonds, which have resilient balance 
sheets and offer attractive yield pick-up. We see the US 10-year 
government bond offering value if the yield rises above 5.25%.  
Dollar appears overbought; potential for gold upside: The 
USD index (DXY) has jumped over 2% in the past two weeks 
after stronger-than-expected US jobs and inflation reports for 
October revived Fed rate hike bets. Nevertheless, crude oil 
prices have slumped almost 20% this month as the Republican 
party’s heavy losses in the US mid-term elections raised the 
prospects of de-escalation in the Middle East conflict. Forward 
markets are pricing US crude oil below USD 75/bbl by Q3 next 
year. Lower oil prices are likely to support disinflation next year. 
With DXY technically overbought and Fed policy expectations 
excessively hawkish, we expect a pullback in USD. Conversely, 
gold looks oversold. We see an upside for XAU/USD, despite 
revising lower our 3- and 12-month targets (see page 7).   
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions 
  How will the new US semiconductor restrictions impact 
the technology sector?  
Our view: While compute is likely to remain a bottleneck for AI, 
greater US regulatory restrictions could weigh on AI capex.   
Rationale: The US has announced broader restrictions on overseas 
computing capacity used by Chinese and other restricted-country 
companies, extending controls beyond physical chip shipments to 
remote access through data centres. This could delay data centre 
deployments and raise financing costs, as affected operators may 
defer expansion plans until licences or replacement customers are 
secured. Lower spending could weigh on chip and hardware orders, 
bringing consensus capex estimates closer to our forecasts. While 
we expect AI capex to rise 50% to USD 1.2trn in 2027, supported 
by projects already under construction, the compound annual growth 
rate (CAGR) between 2027 and 2030 is likely to moderate to c.12%.  
We therefore favour broadening technology exposure beyond 
semiconductors through our US Information Technology (IT) 
opportunistic idea, which also includes hardware and software 
industries. Investors should also consider diversifying into our other 
preferred sectors, such as US healthcare and financials.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  How were Chinese technology giants' Q3 2026 earnings? 
Our view: Chinese tech giants’ Q3 earnings highlight growing 
demand for AI, but heavy spending is pressuring margins and 
cash flow, underscoring the need for stronger AI monetisation. 
Rationale: Heavy AI capex continues to weigh on profitability, 
compressing near-term margins and deepening negative free cash 
flows, with share dilution a risk as tech giants may have to tap into 
equity markets for the AI buildout. Cloud growth points to genuine AI 
demand, but the AI-related revenue contribution remains small 
relative to the cost base. AI monetisation remains challenging, with 
competition set to intensify. Weak consumer spending was a 
common thread contributing to revenue misses. Major players 
reiterated the need for more tangible investment returns amid 
aggressive AI investments. 
In the near term, investor sentiment remains weighed down by 
tightening US restrictions targeting China’s access to advanced 
chips, introducing downside risks to 2027 AI cloud revenue. While 
this could hamper the development of next-generation AI models, 
accelerating domestic AI chip adoption should benefit local 
semiconductor companies as China pushes for technological self-
sufficiency. We expect rising AI adoption to benefit China's IT sector 
over time, though we prefer diversifying our China equity exposure 
across preferred sectors, such as financials and healthcare. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

AI capital expenditure (capex) should continue to 
rise, but growth is expected to moderate from 2028

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the implications of the October Fed meeting 
minutes for the rate path forward and short-dated bonds? Do 
US 10-year government bonds now offer a good re-entry point? 
Our view: October’s Fed meeting minutes maintained a 
hawkish-hold narrative. We continue to expect the Fed to raise 
rates by 25bps at its 8-9 December meeting. However, with 
short-dated US government bonds having largely priced in 
three hikes by June 2027, we see any spike above 5.25% in the 
US 10-year bond yield as an opportunistic bullish entry point.  
Rationale: The hawkish-hold tone of the Fed’s October meeting 
minutes suggests the December Fed meeting is ‘live’. The market is 
currently pricing in three hikes by year-end 2027 (vs. our 
expectation of two hikes by H1 2027 followed by a potential cut 
in H2 2027). Short-dated government bond yields – using the US 2-
year yield as a proxy – have largely priced in three hikes, and we do 
not expect the hawkish minutes to have a material impact on them.   
Should the US 10-year government bond yield spike above 5.25%, 
the near-term risk-reward would become favourable, in our opinion. 
As we expect inflation to moderate in H2 2027, we see scope for 
the Fed to pivot towards easing, and we would turn more 
constructive on short-dated government bonds. We expect the US 
2-year government bond yield to decline to 4.00-4.25% in 12 
months, contributing to yield curve steepening. 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Is it time to add exposure to the EUR after its recent slide 
against the USD? 
Our view: Yes, we are bullish on EUR/USD and favour gradually 
adding EUR exposure in the coming weeks. Markets are pricing 
in more Fed tightening than we expect. Lower oil prices support 
the Euro area and are likely to ease the Fed’s inflation concerns 
on a forward-looking basis. 
Rationale: Lower oil prices are a double positive for EUR/USD, 
as they ease costs for Euro area households and businesses and 
temper US inflation, reducing the need for the Fed to tighten as 
much as markets expect.  
Markets are pricing in a terminal Fed funds rate close to 4.63%, down 
from 4.72% a week ago, with the yield curve now also pricing a rate-
cutting cycle after the September 2027 peak. We expect the bullish 
near-term USD momentum to linger until markets’ peak rate 
expectations converge towards our view. EUR/USD’s relative 
strength index (RSI) is below 30, and consolidation above 1.0950 
points to oversold conditions. We expect a rebound towards 1.11 
to confirm our recovery view in the next three weeks. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What could support a sustained recovery in the AUD? 
Our view: We believe a sustained AUD recovery requires a 
combination of domestic resilience and fading USD momentum. 
Rationale: AUD/USD has been pressured by two key external 
headwinds: hawkish Fed repricing and weaker AI-related risk 
sentiment. A reversal in either could provide scope for recovery. 
Softer US economic data or less hawkish Fed communication could 
ease upward pressure on US government bond yields, narrow rate 
differentials and weaken the USD. At the same time, a stabilisation 
in AI and broader equity sentiment could restore demand for the 
risk-sensitive AUD, particularly if Asian equities recover. 
Domestic fundamentals remain a relative source of support. 
Still-elevated inflation should constrain the RBA’s ability to turn 
dovish quickly, while exporter and real-money demand for the AUD 
could cushion the downside. However, Australia’s rising 
unemployment raises the prospect of RBA rate cuts in H2 2027, 
potentially eroding the AUD’s carry advantage over time. With 
AUD/USD technically oversold, we see scope for a rebound from 
0.6600, with 0.6830 as the key resistance level. 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How will the improving oil supply-demand balance shape 
crude oil prices over the coming quarters? 
Our view: Improving supply-demand balances should support 
inventory rebuilding and ease oil prices over time, despite 
persistent geopolitical risks, supporting disinflation in H2 2027. 
Rationale: Crude oil’s recent retreat marks an important transition: 
expanding physical supply is finally asserting control over 
headline geopolitical noise. With non-OPEC production resilient, 
Gulf exports recovering and alternative transit routes adapting, 
markets are steadily pricing in an approaching supply surplus. 
However, high freight costs and extended voyage times keep 
significant crude oil volumes tied up at sea rather than refilling local 
storage tanks. Crucially, the US Strategic Petroleum Reserve’s 
crude inventories have fallen nearly 33% from pre-conflict levels, 
leaving a thinner buffer against renewed supply disruptions. 
For investors, improving fundamentals point to gradual price 
normalisation as trade routes adapt, despite persistent geopolitical 
volatility. We expect near-term West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil 
to range between USD 80/bbl and USD 90/bbl. Softer energy 
prices should provide some relief to headline inflation, but are 
unlikely to materially alter the Fed’s near-term policy trajectory. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist
